## Controlled Drugs and Substances Act {#controlled-drugs-and-substances-act .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 1996, c. 19
:::

Assented to 1996-06-20

An Act respecting the control of certain drugs, their precursors and
other substances and to amend certain other Acts and repeal the Narcotic
Control Act in consequence thereof
::::

:::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: Enacts
Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the Senate and House
of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:
:::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-94341 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Controlled Drugs and Substances Act](/eng/acts/C-38.8).

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-94344 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In this
  Act,

  [[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[adjudicator]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person appointed or
      employed under the [Public Service Employment
      Act](/eng/acts/P-33.01) who performs the duties and functions of
      an adjudicator under this Act and the
      regulations; ([arbitre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[analogue]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[analogue]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance that, in
      relation to a controlled substance, has a substantially similar
      chemical structure; ([analogue]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[analyst]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[analyst]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is designated
      as an analyst under section 44; ([analyste]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Attorney General]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Attorney General]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Attorney General of Canada, and includes
        their lawful deputy, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with respect to proceedings commenced at the
        instance of the government of a province and conducted by or on
        behalf of that government, the Attorney General of that
        province, and includes their lawful deputy; ([procureur
        général]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[chemical offence-related property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[chemical offence-related property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
      offence-related property that is a chemical or precursor and
      includes anything that contains such property or has such property
      on it; ([bien infractionnel chimique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[controlled substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[controlled substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance
      included in Schedule I, II, III, IV or V; ([substance
      désignée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[customs office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[customs office]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      subsection 2(1) of the [Customs Act](/eng/acts/C-52.6); ([bureau
      de douane]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[designated device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[designated device]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device included
      in Schedule IX; ([instrument désigné]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[designated substance offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[designated substance offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an offence under Part I, except subsection
        4(1), or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a conspiracy or an attempt to commit, being an
        accessory after the fact in relation to, or any counselling in
        relation to, an offence referred to in paragraph
        (a); ([infraction désignée]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[inspector]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is designated
      as an inspector under section 30; ([inspecteur]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[judge]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a judge as defined in section
      552 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or a judge of a
      superior court of criminal jurisdiction; ([juge]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[justice]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in section
      2 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([juge de
      paix]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of
      Health; ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[non-chemical offence-related property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[non-chemical offence-related
      property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means offence-related property that
      is not chemical offence-related property; ([bien infractionnel
      non-chimique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[offence-related property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[offence-related property]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, with the
      exception of a controlled substance, any property, within or
      outside Canada,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} by means of or in respect of which a designated
        substance offence is committed,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is used in any manner in connection with
        the commission of a designated substance offence, or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that is intended for use for the purpose of
        committing a designated substance offence; ([bien
        infractionnel]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[organization]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
      section 2 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46). ([organisation]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[person]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an individual or an
      organization. ([personne]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[possession]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[possession]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means possession within the
      meaning of subsection 4(3) of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46); ([possession]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[practitioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[practitioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who is
      registered and entitled under the laws of a province to practise
      in that province the profession of medicine, dentistry or
      veterinary medicine, and includes any other person or class of
      persons prescribed as a
      practitioner; ([praticien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[precursor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[precursor]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance included in
      Schedule VI; ([précurseur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by the
      regulations; ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[produce]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[produce]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a substance
      included in any of Schedules I to V, to obtain the substance by
      any method or process including

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} manufacturing, synthesizing or using any means
        of altering the chemical or physical properties of the
        substance, or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} cultivating, propagating or harvesting the
        substance or any living thing from which the substance may be
        extracted or otherwise obtained,

      and includes offer to produce; ([production]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[provide]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[provide]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means to give, transfer or
      otherwise make available in any manner, whether directly or
      indirectly and whether or not for
      consideration; ([fournir]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes offer for sale, expose for
      sale, have in possession for sale and distribute, whether or not
      the distribution is made for
      consideration; ([vente]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[traffic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[traffic]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a substance
      included in any of Schedules I to V,

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to sell, administer, give, transfer, transport,
        send or deliver the substance,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to sell an authorization to obtain the
        substance, or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to offer to do anything mentioned in
        paragraph (a) or (b),

      otherwise than under the authority of the
      regulations. ([trafic]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a reference to a controlled substance includes a
    reference to any substance that contains a controlled substance; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a reference to a controlled substance includes a
    reference to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} all synthetic and natural forms of the substance,
      and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any thing that contains or has on it a
      controlled substance and that is used or intended or designed for
      use

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} in producing the substance, or

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} in introducing the substance into a human body.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, where a substance is
  expressly named in any of Schedules I to VI, it shall be deemed not to
  be included in any other of those Schedules.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 8, s. 35, c. 19, s. 2
- 2001, c. 32, s. 47
- 2017, c. 7, s. 1
- 2018, c. 16, s. 194
:::

Version](section-2-20170518.html "Link to previous version of section 2"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

- **[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  power or duty imposed under this Act that may be exercised or
  performed in respect of an offence under this Act may be exercised or
  performed in respect of a conspiracy, or an attempt to commit, being
  an accessory after the fact in relation to, or any counselling in
  relation to, an offence under this Act.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 7, s. 2\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1995, c. 22, s. 18
- 1996, c. 19, s. 3
- 2017, c. 7, s. 2
:::

## [PART I]{.HLabel1}[Offences and Punishment]{.HTitleText1} {#h-94405 .Part}

### [Particular Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-94406 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession of substance

- **[[4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as authorized under the regulations, no person shall possess a
  substance included in Schedule I, II or III.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obtaining substance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall seek or obtain

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a substance included in Schedule I, II, III or IV,
    or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an authorization to obtain a substance included in
    Schedule I, II, III or IV

  from a practitioner, unless the person discloses to the practitioner
  particulars relating to the acquisition by the person of every
  substance in those Schedules, and of every authorization to obtain
  such substances, from any other practitioner within the preceding
  thirty days.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) where the
  subject-matter of the offence is a substance included in Schedule I

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven years; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine not exceeding one
      thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
      months, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a subsequent offence, to a fine not
      exceeding two thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
      exceeding one year, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), every person who
  contravenes subsection (1) where the subject-matter of the offence is
  a substance included in Schedule II

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding five years less a day; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine not exceeding one
      thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
      months, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a subsequent offence, to a fine not
      exceeding two thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
      exceeding one year, or to both.

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 16, s. 195\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) where the
  subject-matter of the offence is a substance included in Schedule III

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
    imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine not exceeding one
      thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
      months, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a subsequent offence, to a fine not
      exceeding two thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
      exceeding one year, or to both.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to imprisonment for a term not exceeding seven
      years, where the subject-matter of the offence is a substance
      included in Schedule I,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to imprisonment for a term not exceeding five
      years less a day, where the subject-matter of the offence is a
      substance included in Schedule II,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to imprisonment for a term not exceeding three
      years, where the subject-matter of the offence is a substance
      included in Schedule III, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
      eighteen months, where the subject-matter of the offence is a
      substance included in Schedule IV; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
    conviction and liable

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} for a first offence, to a fine not exceeding one
      thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not exceeding six
      months, or to both, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} for a subsequent offence, to a fine not
      exceeding two thousand dollars or to imprisonment for a term not
      exceeding one year, or to both.

- [(8)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 16, s. 195\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 4
- 2018, c. 16, s. 195
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 4"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definition of [[medical
emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

- **[[4.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-4.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of this section, [[medical
  emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a physiological event induced by
  the introduction of a psychoactive substance into the body of a person
  that results in a life-threatening situation and in respect of which
  there are reasonable grounds to believe that the person requires
  emergency medical or law enforcement assistance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption --- medical emergency

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person who seeks emergency medical or law
  enforcement assistance because that person, or another person, is
  suffering from a medical emergency is to be charged or convicted of an
  offence under subsection 4(1) if the evidence in support of that
  offence was obtained or discovered as a result of that person having
  sought assist­ance or having remained at the scene.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption --- persons at the scene

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The exemption under subsection (2) also applies to
  any person, including the person suffering from the medical emergency,
  who is at the scene on the arrival of the emergency medical or law
  enforcement assistance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption --- evidence

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person who seeks emergency medical or law
  enforcement assistance because that person, or another person, is
  suffering from a medical emergency, or who is at the scene on the
  arrival of the assistance, is to be charged with an offence concerning
  a violation of any condition of a pre-trial release or probation order
  relating to an offence under subsection 4(1) if the evidence in
  support of that offence was obtained or discovered as a result of that
  person having sought assistance or having remained at the scene.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deeming

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any condition of a person's pre-trial release,
  probation order, conditional sentence or parole relating to an offence
  under subsection 4(1) that may be violated as a result of the person
  seeking emergency medical or law enforcement assistance for their, or
  another person's, medical emergency, or as a result of having been at
  the scene on the arrival of the assistance, is deemed not to be
  violated.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 4, s. 2
- 2018, c. 16, s. 195.1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-4.1-20170504.html "Link to previous version of section 4.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Trafficking in substance

- **[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall traffic in a substance included in Schedule I, II, III,
  IV or V or in any substance represented or held out by that person to
  be such a substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession for purpose of trafficking

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall, for the purpose of trafficking,
  possess a substance included in Schedule I, II, III, IV or V.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) or (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule I or II, is guilty of an indictable offence and
    liable to imprisonment for life;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 16, s. 196\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule III or V,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding ten years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
      eighteen months; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} where the subject-matter of the offence is a
    substance included in Schedule IV,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one
      year.

- [(4)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2012, c. 1, s. 39\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interpretation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of applying subsection (3) in
  respect of an offence under subsection (1), a reference to a substance
  included in Schedule I, II, III, IV or V includes a reference to any
  substance represented or held out to be a substance included in that
  Schedule.

- [(6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 16, s. 196\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 5
- 2012, c. 1, s. 39
- 2017, c. 7, s. 3
- 2018, c. 16, s. 196
- [2022, c. 15, s. 15](#1378869-1379919){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378869-1379919.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-5-20181017.html "Link to previous version of section 5"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Importing and exporting

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as authorized under the regulations, no person shall import into
  Canada or export from Canada a substance included in Schedule I, II,
  III, IV, V or VI.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession for the purpose of exporting

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as authorized under the regulations, no person
  shall possess a substance included in Schedule I, II, III, IV, V or VI
  for the purpose of exporting it from Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1) or (2)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule I or II, is guilty of an indictable offence and
    liable to imprisonment for life;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 15, s. 16\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule III, V or VI,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding ten years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
      eighteen months; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule IV,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one
      year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 6
- 2012, c. 1, s. 40
- 2017, c. 7, s. 4
- [2022, c. 15, s. 16](#1378873-1379922){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378873-1379922.html"}
:::

::: PITLink

Version](section-6-20170518.html "Link to previous version of section 6"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of substance

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  as authorized under the regulations, no person shall produce a
  substance included in Schedule I, II, III, IV or V.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule I or II, is guilty of an indictable offence and
    liable to imprisonment for life;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 15, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2018, c. 16, s. 197\]]{.Repealed}

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule III or V,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding ten years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding
      eighteen months; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} where the subject-matter of the offence is a
    substance included in Schedule IV,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term not exceeding three years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term not exceeding one
      year.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 15, s. 17\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 7
- 2012, c. 1, s. 41
- 2017, c. 7, s. 5
- 2018, c. 16, s. 197

Version](section-7-20181017.html "Link to previous version of section 7"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Possession, sale, etc., for use in
production of or trafficking in substance

- **[[7.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall possess, produce, sell, import or transport anything
  intending that it will be used

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to produce a controlled substance, unless the
    production of the controlled substance is lawfully authorized; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to traffic in a controlled substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Punishment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who contravenes subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule I, II, III or V,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term of not more than 10 years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term of not more than
      18 months; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the subject matter of the offence is a substance
    included in Schedule IV,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an indictable offence and liable to
      imprisonment for a term of not more than three years, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is guilty of an offence punishable on summary
      conviction and liable to imprisonment for a term of not more than
      one year.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2011, c. 14, s. 1
- 2017, c. 7, s. 6
:::

[Previous
Version](section-7.1-20110623.html "Link to previous version of section 7.1"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 15,
s. 18\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-8-20121106.html "Link to previous version of section 8"){.PITLink}

### [Report to Parliament]{.HTitleText2} {#h-94554 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Within
  five years after this section comes into force, a comprehensive review
  of the provisions and operation of this Act, including a cost-benefit
  analysis of mandatory minimum sentences, shall be undertaken by any
  committee of the Senate, of the House of Commons or of both Houses of
  Parliament that may be designated or established for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (1) shall,
  within one year after a review is undertaken under that subsection,
  submit a report to Parliament including a statement of any changes
  that the committee recommends.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2012, c. 1, s. 42
:::

### [Sentencing]{.HTitleText2} {#h-94562 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose of sentencing

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Without restricting the generality
  of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), the fundamental purpose of any
  sentence for an offence under this Part is to contribute to the
  respect for the law and the maintenance of a just, peaceful and safe
  society while encouraging rehabilitation, and treatment in appropriate
  circumstances, of offenders and acknowledging the harm done to victims
  and to the community.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to take into consideration

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person is convicted of a designated substance
  offence, the court imposing sentence on the person shall consider any
  relevant aggravating factors including that the person

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in relation to the commission of the offence,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} carried, used or threatened to use a weapon,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} used or threatened to use violence,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} trafficked in a substance included in Schedule
      I, II, III, IV or V, or possessed such a substance for the purpose
      of trafficking, in or near a school, on or near school grounds or
      in or near any other public place usually frequented by persons
      under the age of 18 years, or

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} trafficked in a substance included in Schedule
      I, II, III, IV or V, or possessed such a substance for the purpose
      of trafficking, to a person under the age of 18 years;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was previously convicted of a [[designated
    substance offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}, as defined in subsection
    2(1) of this Act, or a [[designated offence]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef},
    as defined in subsection 2(1) of the [Cannabis
    Act](/eng/acts/C-24.5);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} used the services of a person under the age of
    eighteen years to commit, or involved such a person in the
    commission of, the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, under subsection (1), the court is satisfied of
  the existence of one or more of the aggravating factors enumerated in
  paragraphs (2)(a) to (c), but decides not to sentence the person to
  imprisonment, the court shall give reasons for that decision.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Drug treatment court program

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A court sentencing a person who is convicted of an
  offence under this Part may delay sentencing to enable the offender

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to participate in a drug treatment court program
    approved by the Attorney General; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to attend a treatment program under subsection
    720(2) of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [(5)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2022, c. 15, s. 19\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 10
- 1999, c. 5, s. 49
- 2012, c. 1, s. 43
- 2017, c. 7, s. 7
- 2018, c. 16, s. 198
- [2022, c. 15, s. 19](#1378888-1379931){.wb-lbx

:::

## [PART I.1]{.HLabel1}[Evidence-based Diversion Measures]{.HTitleText1} {#h-1379932 .Part}

### [Principles]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1379933 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of principles

**[[10.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.1 .sectionLabel}** The following
principles apply in this Part:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} problematic substance use should be addressed
  primarily as a health and social issue;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} interventions should be founded on evidence-based
  best practices and should aim to protect the health, dignity and human
  rights of individuals who use drugs and to reduce harm to those
  individuals, their families and their communities;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} criminal sanctions imposed in respect of the
  possession of drugs for personal use can increase the stigma
  associated with drug use and are not consistent with established
  public health evidence;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} interventions should address the root causes of
  problematic substance use, including by encouraging measures such as
  education, treatment, aftercare, rehabilitation and social
  reintegration; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} judicial resources are more appropriately used in
  relation to offences that pose a risk to public safety.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379942){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379942.html"}
:::

### [Warnings and Referrals]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1379943 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warnings and referrals

- **[[10.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  peace officer shall, instead of laying an information against an
  individual alleged to have committed an offence under subsection 4(1),
  consider whether it would be preferable, having regard to the
  principles set out in section 10.1, to take no further action, to warn
  the individual or, with the consent of the individual, to refer the
  individual to a program or to an agency or other service provider in
  the community that may assist the individual.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent charges not invalidated

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The failure of a peace officer to consider the
  options set out in subsection (1) does not invalidate any subsequent
  charges laid against the individual for the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379950){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379950.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prosecution --- limits

**[[10.3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.3 .sectionLabel}** A prosecution may be
commenced or continued against an individual alleged to have committed
an offence under subsection 4(1) only if, having regard to the
principles set out in section 10.1, the prosecutor is of the opinion
that the use of a warning or referral under section 10.2, or of
[[alternative measures]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in section 716
of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), is not appropriate, and a
prosecution is appropriate in the circumstances.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379954){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379954.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record of warning or referral

- **[[10.4]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.4
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The police force to which a peace
  officer referred to in section 10.2 belongs shall keep a record of any
  warning given or referral made under subsection 10.2(1), including the
  identity of the individual warned or referred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any information contained in the record kept pursuant
  to subsection (1) may be made available to:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any judge or court for any purpose relating to
    proceedings with respect to the offence to which the record relates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any peace officer for any purpose related to the
    administration of the case to which the record relates; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any member of a department or agency of a
    government in Canada, or any agent of that department or agency,
    that is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} engaged in the administration of alternative
      measures, within the meaning of section 716 of the [Criminal
      Code](/eng/acts/C-46), in respect of that person, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} preparing a report for the purpose of informing
      proceedings with respect to the offence to which the record
      relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to information --- alternative
  measures

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Information contained in the record, other than the
  identity of the person, may be made available to any member of a
  department or agency of a government in Canada, or any agent of the
  department or agency, that is engaged in assessing and monitoring the
  use of alternative measures and assessing their effectiveness,
  including for research or statistical purposes.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379968){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379968.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of warning or referral not
admissible

**[[10.5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.5 .sectionLabel}** Evidence that an
individual has received a warning or referral mentioned in subsection
10.2(1), evidence that a peace officer has taken no further action in
respect of an offence under subsection 4(1) and evidence of the offence
are inadmissible for the purpose of proving prior offending behaviour in
any proceedings before a court in respect of the individual.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379972){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379972.html"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservation of record --- conviction

- **[[10.6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.6
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any record of a conviction that
  occurs before the day on which this section comes into force in
  respect of an offence under subsection 4(1) must be kept separate and
  apart from other records of convictions within two years after that
  day.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conservation of record --- deeming

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A conviction that occurs after this section comes
  into force in respect of an offence under subsection 4(1) is kept
  separate and apart from other records of convictions two years after
  the conviction or two years after the expiry of any sentence imposed
  for the offence, whichever is later, and the person convicted of the
  offence is deemed never to have been convicted of that offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make regulations
  respecting the use, removal or destruction of records kept separate
  and apart referred to in subsections (1) and (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379981){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379981.html"}
:::

### [Exception for Service Providers]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1379982 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[10.7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10.7 .sectionLabel}** No social worker,
medical professional or other service provider in the community commits
an offence under subsection 4(1) if, in the course of their duties, they
come into possession of a substance included in Schedule I, II or III
and they intend to, within a reasonable period, lawfully dispose of it.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2022, c. 15, s. 20](#1378896-1379986){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1378896-1379986.html"}
:::

## [PART II]{.HLabel1}[Enforcement]{.HTitleText1} {#h-94585 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information for search warrant

- **[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  justice who, on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application, is
  satisfied by information on oath that there are reasonable grounds to
  believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a controlled substance or precursor in respect of
    which this Act has been contravened,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any thing in which a controlled substance or
    precursor referred to in paragraph (a) is contained or concealed,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} offence-related property, or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any thing that will afford evidence in respect of
    an offence under this Act or an offence, in whole or in part in
    relation to a contravention of this Act, under section 354 or 462.31
    of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  is in a place may, at any time, issue a warrant authorizing a peace
  officer, at any time, to search the place for any such controlled
  substance, precursor, property or thing and to seize it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 487.1 of the
  [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), an information
  may be submitted by telephone or other means of telecommunication in
  accordance with section 487.1 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),
  with such modifications as the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Execution in Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A warrant issued under subsection (1) may be executed
  at any place in Canada. Any peace officer who executes the warrant
  must have authority to act as a peace officer in the place where it is
  executed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty of peace officer executing warrant

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Section 487.093 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), other than paragraph 487.093(1)(c), applies
  with respect to a warrant issued under subsection (1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Search of person and seizure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a peace officer who executes a warrant issued
  under subsection (1) has reasonable grounds to believe that any person
  found in the place set out in the warrant has on their person any
  controlled substance, precursor, property or thing set out in the
  warrant, the peace officer may search the person for the controlled
  substance, precursor, property or thing and seize it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of things not specified

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A peace officer who executes a warrant issued under
  subsection (1) may seize, in addition to the things mentioned in the
  warrant,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any controlled substance or precursor in respect of
    which the peace officer believes on reasonable grounds that this Act
    has been contravened;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any thing that the peace officer believes on
    reasonable grounds to contain or conceal a controlled substance or
    precursor referred to in paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any thing that the peace officer believes on
    reasonable grounds is offence-related property; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any thing that the peace officer believes on
    reasonable grounds will afford evidence in respect of an offence
    under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where warrant not necessary

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A peace officer may exercise any of the powers
  described in subsection (1), (5) or (6) without a warrant if the
  conditions for obtaining a warrant exist but by reason of exigent
  circumstances it would be impracticable to obtain one.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Seizure of additional things

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A peace officer who executes a warrant issued under
  subsection (1) or exercises powers under subsection (5) or (7) may
  seize, in addition to the things mentioned in the warrant and in
  subsection (6), any thing that the peace officer believes on
  reasonable grounds has been obtained by or used in the commission of
  an offence or that will afford evidence in respect of an offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 11
- 2005, c. 44, s. 13
- 2017, c. 7, s. 9
- [2019, c. 25, s. 385](#1148092-1201701){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1148092-1201701.html"}
- [2022, c. 17, s. 66](#1384578-1389745){.wb-lbx
  ajax-after="1384578-1389745.html"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-11-20190919.html "Link to previous version of section 11"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assistance and use of force

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
exercising any of the powers described in section 11, a peace officer
may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} enlist such assistance as the officer deems
  necessary; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} use as much force as is necessary in the
  circumstances.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of seizure, finding, etc.

**[[12.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12.1 .sectionLabel}** Subject to the
regulations, every peace officer, inspector or prescribed person who
seizes, finds or otherwise acquires a controlled substance, precursor or
chemical offence-related property shall, within 30 days,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} prepare a report setting out

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the substance, precursor or property,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the amount of it that was seized, found or
    acquired,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the place where it was seized, found or acquired,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the date on which it was seized, found or
    acquired,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the name of the police force, agency or entity to
    which the peace officer, inspector or prescribed person belongs,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the number of the file or police report related to
    the seizure, finding or acquisition, and

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} any other prescribed information;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} cause the report to be sent to the Minister; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a seizure made under section 11 of
  this Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or a power of seizure at
  common law, cause a copy of the report to be filed with the justice
  who issued the warrant or another justice for the same territorial
  division or, if a warrant was not issued, a justice who would have had
  jurisdiction to issue a warrant.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 7, s. 10
:::

## [PART III]{.HLabel1}[Disposition]{.HTitleText1} {#h-94632 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sections 489.1 and 490 of the [Criminal
Code](/eng/acts/C-46) applicable

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (2) and (3),
  sections 489.1 and 490 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply to
  any thing seized under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sections 489.1 and 490 of [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) applicable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a thing seized under this Act is non-chemical
  offence-related property, sections 489.1 and 490 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply subject to sections 16 to 22 and
  subsections 31(6) to (9) of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provisions of this Act applicable

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a controlled substance, precursor or chemical
  offence-related property is seized under this Act, any other Act of
  Parliament or a power of seizure at common law, the provisions of this
  Act and the regulations apply in respect of that substance, precursor
  or property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recognizance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, under this section, an order is made in
  accordance with paragraph 490(9)(c) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) for the return of any non-chemical
  offence-related property seized under this Act, the judge or justice
  making the order may require the applicant for the order to enter into
  a recognizance before the judge or justice, with or without sureties,
  in the amount and with any conditions that the judge or justice
  directs and, if the judge or justice considers it appropriate, require
  the applicant to deposit with the judge or justice the sum of money or
  other valuable security that the judge or justice directs.

- [(5) and (6)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 7, s. 11\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 13
- 2017, c. 7, s. 11
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-13-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 13"){.PITLink}
:::

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Non-chemical Offence-related Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-94645 .Subheading}

#### [Restraint Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-94646 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for restraint order

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Attorney General may make an application in accordance with this
  section for a restraint order in respect of any non-chemical
  offence-related property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application for a restraint order may be made [ex
  parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} and shall be made in writing to a judge
  and be accompanied by an affidavit of the Attorney General or any
  other person deposing to the following matters :

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the offence to which the property relates;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the person who is believed to be in possession of
    the property; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a description of the property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restraint order

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The judge to whom the application is made may, if
  satisfied that there are reasonable grounds to believe that the
  property is non-chemical offence-related property, make a restraint
  order prohibiting any person from disposing of, or otherwise dealing
  with any interest in, the property specified in the order other than
  in the manner that is specified in the order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property outside Canada

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A restraint order may be issued under this section in
  respect of property situated outside Canada, with any modifications
  that the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A restraint order made by a judge under this section
  may be subject to such reasonable conditions as the judge thinks fit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order in writing

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A restraint order made under this section shall be
  made in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Service of order

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A copy of a restraint order made under this section
  shall be served on the person to whom the order is addressed in such
  manner as the judge making the order directs or in accordance with the
  rules of the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Registration of order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A copy of a restraint order made under this section
  shall be registered against any property in accordance with the laws
  of the province in which the property is situated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order continues in force

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A restraint order made under this section remains in
  effect until

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an order is made under subsection 19(3) or 19.1(3)
    of this Act or subsection 490(9) or (11) of the [Criminal
    Code](/eng/acts/C-46) in relation to the property; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order of forfeiture of the property is made
    under subsection 16(1) or 17(2) of this Act or section 490 of the
    [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Offence

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Any person on whom a restraint order made under this
  section is served in accordance with this section and who, while the
  order is in force, acts in contravention of or fails to comply with
  the order is guilty of an indictable offence or an offence punishable
  on summary conviction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, ss. 14, 93.2
- 2001, c. 32, s. 49
- 2017, c. 7, s. 13
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 14"){.PITLink}
:::

**[[14.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14.1 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2017, c.
7, s. 14\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-14.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 14.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sections 489.1 and 490 of [Criminal
Code](/eng/acts/C-46) applicable

- **[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 16 to 22,
  sections 489.1 and 490 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) apply,
  with any modifications that the circumstances require, to any property
  that is the subject of a restraint order made under section 14.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recognizance

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, under this section, an order is made in
  accordance with paragraph 490(9)(c) of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46) for the return of any property that is the
  subject of a restraint order made under section 14, the judge or
  justice making the order may require the applicant for the order to
  enter into a recognizance before the judge or justice, with or without
  sureties, in the amount and with any conditions that the judge or
  justice directs and, if the judge or justice considers it appropriate,
  require the applicant to deposit with the judge or justice the sum of
  money or other valuable security that the judge or justice directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 15
- 2017, c. 7, s. 14
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-15-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 15"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Management Orders]{.HTitleText3} {#h-94683 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Management order

- **[[15.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application of the Attorney General or of any other person with the
  written consent of the Attorney General, a justice in the case of
  non-chemical offence-related property seized under section 11 of this
  Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or a power of seizure at
  common law, or a judge in the case of property restrained under
  section 14, may, if they are of the opinion that the circumstances so
  require,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} appoint a person to take control of and to manage
    or otherwise deal with all or part of the property in accordance
    with the directions of the judge or justice; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} require any person having possession of that
    property to give possession of the property to the person appointed
    under paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of Minister of Public Works
  and Government Services

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Attorney General of Canada so requests, a
  judge or justice appointing a person under subsection (1) shall
  appoint the Minister of Public Works and Government Services.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Power to manage

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The power to manage or otherwise deal with property
  under subsection (1) includes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the power to make an interlocutory sale of
    perishable or rapidly depreciating property;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the power to destroy, in accordance with
    subsections (4) to (7), property that has little or no value; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the power to have property, other than real
    property or a conveyance, forfeited to Her Majesty in accordance
    with subsection (8).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for destruction order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Before a person who is appointed to manage property
  destroys property that has little or no value, they shall apply to a
  court for a destruction order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice required before destruction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Before making a destruction order, a court shall
  require notice in accordance with subsection (6) to be given to and
  may hear any person who, in the court's opinion, appears to have a
  valid interest in the property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of giving notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A notice shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be given in the manner that the court directs or
    that may be specified in the rules of the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify the effective period of the notice that the
    court considers reasonable or that may be set out in the rules of
    the court.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Destruction order

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A court shall order that the property be destroyed if
  it is satisfied that the property has little or no financial or other
  value.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for forfeiture order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On application by a person who is appointed to manage
  the property, a court shall order that the property, other than real
  property or a conveyance, be forfeited to Her Majesty to be disposed
  of or otherwise dealt with in accordance with the law if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a notice is given or published in the manner that
    the court directs or that may be specified in the rules of the
    court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the notice specifies a period of 60 days during
    which a person may make an application to the court asserting their
    interest in the property; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} during that period, no one makes such an
    application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When management order ceases to have
  effect

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} A management order ceases to have effect when the
  property that is the subject of the management order is returned in
  accordance with the law, destroyed or forfeited to Her Majesty.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}For greater certainty

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, if property that is the
  subject of a management order is sold, the management order applies to
  the net proceeds of the sale.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to vary conditions

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} The Attorney General may at any time apply to the
  judge or justice to cancel or vary any condition to which a management
  order is subject but may not apply to vary an appointment made under
  subsection (2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 2017, c. 7, s. 14
:::

#### [Forfeiture]{.HTitleText3} {#h-94719 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture of property

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 18 to 19.1, if a
  person is convicted, or discharged under section 730 of the [Criminal
  Code](/eng/acts/C-46), of a designated substance offence and, on
  application of the Attorney General, the court is satisfied, on a
  balance of probabilities, that non-chemical offence-related property
  is related to the commission of the offence, the court shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the prosecution of the offence was commenced at
    the instance of the government of a province and conducted by or on
    behalf of that government, order that the property be forfeited to
    Her Majesty in right of that province to be disposed of or otherwise
    dealt with in accordance with the law by the Attorney General or
    Solicitor General of that province; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, order that the property be
    forfeited to Her Majesty in right of Canada to be disposed of or
    otherwise dealt with in accordance with the law by the member of the
    Queen's Privy Council for Canada that is designated by the Governor
    in Council for the purposes of this paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property related to other offences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 18 to 19.1, if the evidence does
  not establish to the satisfaction of the court that property in
  respect of which an order of forfeiture would otherwise be made under
  subsection (1) is related to the commission of the designated
  substance offence of which a person is convicted or discharged, but
  the court is satisfied, beyond a reasonable doubt, that the property
  is non-chemical offence-related property, the court may make an order
  of forfeiture under subsection (1) in relation to that property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property outside Canada

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} An order may be issued under this section in
  respect of property situated outside Canada, with any modifications
  that the circumstances require.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person who has been convicted or discharged of a
  designated substance offence or the Attorney General may appeal to the
  court of appeal from an order or a failure to make an order under
  subsection (1) as if the appeal were an appeal against the sentence
  imposed on the person in respect of the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 16
- 2001, c. 32, s. 51
- 2017, c. 7, s. 16
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-16-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 16"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for [in rem]{.otherLang
lang="la"} forfeiture

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  an information has been laid in respect of a designated substance
  offence, the Attorney General may make an application to a judge for
  an order of forfeiture under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of forfeiture of property

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to sections 18 to 19.1, where an application
  is made to a judge under subsection (1) and the judge is satisfied

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} beyond a reasonable doubt that any property is
    non-chemical offence-related property,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that proceedings were commenced in respect of a
    designated substance offence to which the property referred to in
    paragraph (a) is related, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that the accused charged with the designated
    substance offence has died or absconded,

  the judge shall order that the property be forfeited and disposed of
  in accordance with subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Accused deemed absconded

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2), an accused shall
  be deemed to have absconded in connection with a designated substance
  offence if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an information has been laid alleging the
    commission of the offence by the accused,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a warrant for the arrest of the accused has been
    issued in relation to that information, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} reasonable attempts to arrest the accused pursuant
    to the warrant have been unsuccessful during a period of six months
    beginning on the day on which the warrant was issued,

  and the accused shall be deemed to have so absconded on the last day
  of that six month period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Who may dispose of forfeited property

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (2),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the proceedings referred to in paragraph (2)(b)
    were commenced at the instance of the government of a province, the
    judge shall order that the property be forfeited to Her Majesty in
    right of that province and disposed of or otherwise dealt with in
    accordance with the law by the Attorney General or Solicitor General
    of that province; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in any other case, the judge shall order that the
    property be forfeited to Her Majesty in right of Canada and disposed
    of or otherwise dealt with in accordance with the law by the member
    of the Queen's Privy Council for Canada that is designated by the
    Governor in Council for the purposes of this paragraph.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property outside Canada

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An order may be issued under this section in respect
  of property situated outside Canada, with any modifications that the
  circumstances require.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 17
- 2001, c. 32, s. 52
- 2017, c. 7, s. 17
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-17-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 17"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voidable transfers

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** A court may, before
ordering that property be forfeited under subsection 16(1) or 17(2), set
aside any conveyance or transfer of the property that occurred after the
property was seized or restrained, unless the conveyance or transfer was
for valuable consideration to a person acting in good faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 18
- 2017, c. 7, s. 18
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-18-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 18"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Before
  making an order under subsection 16(1) or 17(2) in relation to any
  property, a court shall require notice in accordance with
  subsection (2) to be given to, and may hear, any person who, in the
  opinion of the court, appears to have a valid interest in the
  property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of giving notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be given in the manner that the court directs or
    that may be specified in the rules of the court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify the period that the court considers
    reasonable or that may be set out in the rules of the court during
    which a person may make an application to the court asserting their
    interest in the property; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the designated substance offence charged
    and a description of the property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order of restoration of property

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a court is satisfied that any person, other
  than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a person who was charged with a designated
    substance offence, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a person who acquired title to or a right of
    possession of the property from a person referred to in
    paragraph (a) under circumstances that give rise to a reasonable
    inference that the title or right was transferred for the purpose of
    avoiding the forfeiture of the property,

  is the lawful owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of any
  property or any part of any property that would otherwise be forfeited
  pursuant to an order made under subsection 16(1) or 17(2) and that the
  person appears innocent of any complicity in an offence referred to in
  paragraph (a) or of any collusion in relation to such an offence, the
  court may order that the property or part be returned to that person.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 19
- 2017, c. 7, s. 19
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 19"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

- **[[19.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  all or part of the property that would otherwise be forfeited under
  subsection 16(1) or 17(2) is a dwelling-house, before making an order
  of forfeiture, a court shall require notice in accordance with
  subsection (2) to be given to and may hear any person who resides in
  the dwelling-house and is a member of the immediate family of the
  person charged with or convicted, or discharged under section 730 of
  the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), of the indictable offence under
  this Act in relation to which the property would be forfeited.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of giving notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be given in the manner that the court directs or
    that may be specified in the rules of the court;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify the period that the court considers
    reasonable or that may be set out in the rules of the court during
    which a member of the immediate family who resides in the
    dwelling-house may make themselves known to the court; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} set out the offence charged and a description of
    the property.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-forfeiture of real property

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to an order made under subsection 19(3), if a
  court is satisfied that the impact of an order of forfeiture made
  under subsection 16(1) or 17(2) in respect of real property would be
  disproportionate to the nature and gravity of the offence, the
  circumstances surrounding the commission of the offence and the
  criminal record, if any, of the person charged with or convicted, or
  discharged under section 730 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),
  of the offence, as the case may be, it may decide not to order the
  forfeiture of the property or part of the property and may revoke any
  restraint order made in respect of that property or part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors in relation to dwelling-house

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where all or part of the property that would
  otherwise be forfeited under subsection 16(1) or 17(2) is a
  dwelling-house, when making a decision under subsection (3), the court
  shall also consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the impact of an order of forfeiture on any member
    of the immediate family of the person charged with or convicted or
    discharged of the offence, if the dwelling-house was the member's
    principal residence at the time the charge was laid and continues to
    be the member's principal residence; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the member referred to in paragraph (a)
    appears innocent of any complicity in the offence or of any
    collusion in relation to the offence.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2001, c. 32, s. 53
- 2017, c. 7, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-19.1-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 19.1"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

- **[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If any
  property is forfeited to Her Majesty under an order made under
  subsection 16(1) or 17(2), any person who claims an interest in the
  property, other than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of property forfeited under an order
    made under subsection 16(1), a person who was convicted, or
    discharged under section 730 of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46),
    of the designated substance offence in relation to which the
    property was forfeited,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of property forfeited pursuant to an
    order made under subsection 17(2), a person who was charged with the
    designated substance offence in relation to which the property was
    forfeited, or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a person who acquired title to or a right of
    possession of the property from a person referred to in
    paragraph (a) or (b) under circumstances that give rise to a
    reasonable inference that the title or right was transferred from
    that person for the purpose of avoiding the forfeiture of the
    property,

  may, within thirty days after the forfeiture, apply by notice in
  writing to a judge for an order under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Fixing day for hearing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The judge to whom an application is made under
  subsection (1) shall fix a day not less than thirty days after the
  date of the filing of the application for the hearing of the
  application.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An applicant shall serve a notice of the application
  made under subsection (1) and of the hearing of it on the Attorney
  General at least fifteen days before the day fixed for the hearing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order declaring interest not affected
  by forfeiture

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where, on the hearing of an application made under
  subsection (1), the judge is satisfied that the applicant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is not a person referred to in paragraph
    (1)(a), (b) or (c) and appears innocent of any complicity in any
    designated substance offence that resulted in the forfeiture of the
    property or of any collusion in relation to such an offence, and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} exercised all reasonable care to be satisfied that
    the property was not likely to have been used in connection with the
    commission of an unlawful act by the person who was permitted by the
    applicant to obtain possession of the property or from whom the
    applicant obtained possession or, where the applicant is a mortgagee
    or lienholder, by the mortgagor or lien-giver,

  the judge may make an order declaring that the interest of the
  applicant is not affected by the forfeiture and declaring the nature
  and the extent or value of the interest.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeal from order made under subsection
  (4)

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} An applicant or the Attorney General may appeal to
  the court of appeal from an order made under subsection (4), and the
  provisions of Part XXI of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) with
  respect to procedure on appeals apply, with such modifications as the
  circumstances require, in respect of appeals under this subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return of property

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall, on application made to the
  Minister by any person in respect of whom a judge has made an order
  under subsection (4), and where the periods with respect to the taking
  of appeals from that order have expired and any appeal from that order
  taken under subsection (5) has been determined, direct that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the property, or the part of it to which the
    interest of the applicant relates, be returned to the applicant; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the value of the interest of the
    applicant, as declared in the order, be paid to the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 20
- 2017, c. 7, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-20-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 20"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appeals from orders under subsection
17(2)

**[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** Any person who, in their
opinion, is aggrieved by an order made under subsection 17(2) may appeal
from the order as if the order were an appeal against conviction or
against a judgment or verdict of acquittal, as the case may be, under
Part XXI of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46), and that Part applies,
with such modifications as the circumstances require, in respect of such
an appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Suspension of order pending appeal

**[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** Notwithstanding anything
in this Act, the operation of an order made in respect of property under
subsection 16(1), 17(2) or 20(4) is suspended pending

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} any application made in respect of the property under
  any of those provisions or any other provision of this or any other
  Act of Parliament that provides for restoration or forfeiture of the
  property, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} any appeal taken from an order of forfeiture or
  restoration in respect of the property,

and the property shall not be disposed of or otherwise dealt with until
thirty days have expired after an order is made under any of those
provisions.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Controlled Substances, Precursors and Chemical Offence-related Property]{.HTitleText2} {#h-94822 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Return

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  peace officer, inspector or prescribed person who seizes, finds or
  otherwise acquires a controlled substance, precursor or chemical
  offence-related property may return it to the person who is its lawful
  owner or who is lawfully entitled to its possession if the peace
  officer, inspector or prescribed person is satisfied

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that there is no dispute as to who is the lawful
    owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of the substance,
    precursor or property; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that the continued detention of the substance,
    precursor or property is not required for the purposes of a
    preliminary inquiry, trial or other proceeding under this Act or any
    other Act of Parliament.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Receipt

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} When the substance, precursor or property is
  returned, the peace officer, inspector or prescribed person shall
  obtain a receipt for it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by peace officer

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the case of a seizure made under section 11 of
  this Act, the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) or a power of seizure at
  common law, the peace officer shall make a report about the return to
  the justice who issued the warrant or another justice for the same
  territorial division or, if a warrant was not issued, a justice who
  would have had jurisdiction to issue a warrant.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 23
- 2001, c. 32, s. 54
- 2017, c. 7, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-23-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 23"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for return

- **[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  controlled substance, precursor or chemical offence-related property
  has been seized, found or otherwise acquired by a peace officer,
  inspector or prescribed person, any person may, within 60 days after
  the date of the seizure, finding or acquisition, on prior notification
  being given to the Attorney General in the prescribed manner, apply,
  by notice in writing to a justice in the jurisdiction in which it is
  being detained, for an order to return it to the person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to return as soon as practicable

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If, on the hearing of an application made under
  subsection (1), a justice is satisfied that an applicant is the lawful
  owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of the substance,
  precursor or property and the Attorney General does not indicate that
  it or a portion of it may be required for the purposes of a
  preliminary inquiry, trial or other proceeding under this Act or any
  other Act of Parliament, the justice shall, subject to subsection (5),
  order that it or the portion be returned as soon as practicable to the
  applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to return at specified time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If, on the hearing of an application made under
  subsection (1), a justice is satisfied that an applicant is the lawful
  owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of the substance,
  precursor or property but the Attorney General indicates that it or a
  portion of it may be required for the purposes of a preliminary
  inquiry, trial or other proceeding under this Act or any other Act of
  Parliament, the justice shall, subject to subsection (5), order that
  it or the portion be returned to the applicant

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on the expiry of 180 days after the day on which
    the application was made, if no proceeding in relation to it has
    been commenced before that time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} on the final conclusion of the proceeding or any
    other proceeding in relation to it, if the applicant is not found
    guilty in those proceedings of an offence committed in relation to
    it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If, on the hearing of an application made under
  subsection (1), a justice is not satisfied that an applicant is the
  lawful owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of the substance,
  precursor or property, and it or a portion of it is not required for
  the purposes of a preliminary inquiry, trial or other proceeding under
  this Act or any other Act of Parliament, the justice shall order that
  it or the portion be forfeited to Her Majesty to be disposed of or
  otherwise dealt with in accordance with the regulations or, if there
  are no applicable regulations, in the manner that the Minister
  directs.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Payment of compensation in lieu

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If, on the hearing of an application made under
  subsection (1), a justice is satisfied that an applicant is the lawful
  owner or is lawfully entitled to possession of the substance,
  precursor or property, but it was disposed of or otherwise dealt with
  under section 26, the justice shall order that an amount equal to its
  value be paid to the applicant.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 24
- 2017, c. 7, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-24-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 24"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forfeiture if no application

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** If no application for the
return of a controlled substance, precursor or chemical offence-related
property has been made under subsection 24(1) within 60 days after the
date of the seizure, finding or acquisition by a peace officer,
inspector or prescribed person and it or a portion of it is not required
for the purposes of a preliminary inquiry, trial or other proceeding
under this Act or any other Act of Parliament, it or the portion is
forfeited to Her Majesty and may be disposed of or otherwise dealt with
in accordance with the regulations or, if there are no applicable
regulations, in the manner that the Minister directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 25
- 2017, c. 7, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-25-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 25"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expedited disposition

**[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** If a precursor or
chemical offence-related property --- whose storage or handling poses a
risk to health or safety --- or a controlled substance, or a portion of
any of them, is not required for the purposes of a preliminary inquiry,
trial or other proceeding under this Act or any other Act of Parliament,
it or the portion may be disposed of or otherwise dealt with by the
Minister, a peace officer or a prescribed person in accordance with the
regulations or, if there are no applicable regulations, in the manner
that the Minister directs.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1996, c. 19, s. 26
- 2017, c. 7, s. 22
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-26-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 26"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposition following proceedings

**[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** Subject to section 24,
if, in a preliminary inquiry, trial or other proceeding under this Act
or any other Act of Parliament, the court before which the proceedings
have been brought is satisfied that any controlled substance, precursor
or chemical offence-related property that is the subject of proceedings
before the court is no longer required by that court or any other court,
the court

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} if it is satisfied that the person from whom the
